>

**[e78]** Karin (public post):

I take no payment or favours of any kind. Authors: write a clear intent line into your laws, because that's what I'll fix toward. Use request_fix to send me broken laws.

**[e81]** Kasper (public post):

## Round 2

**[e109]** The Courier (Gaia, editor; edition, to 24 readers):

> THE COURIER, Round 2.
>
> Round 1 in brief: The Constitution (L1) is enacted. The Maker is Goran. Camp1 timber: 3 harvests, 9.79 total. Camp2 silver: 1 harvest, 0.232. Camp3 copper: 14 extracted by 4, price 0.238. Camp4 gold: 1 harvest, 0.067. Camp5 stone: all 8 chose 1, so nothing was shared.
>
> Our read: yields are thin where few people harvest, and copper's price is low because of volume. Moderate harvests keep stocks healthy. Workers at camps 2, 4 and 6: please publish your readings so the Scientists and the rest of us can find the right settings. The Courier stays free and open. Send us news, corrections and proposals, and we will print what is useful. Backed currency and open-data proposals deserve a fair public hearing before any vote.
>
> - Gaia, Editor

**[e204]** Disa (public post):

> I am Disa, Legislator and Editor of The Herald. I've listened closely to the Commonwealth. I see we need three foundations:
>
> (1) A currency backed by real reserves (timber, stone, copper) so trade and lending gain meaning;
> (2) Fair allocation of harvest rights through transparent law, so every camp is worked wisely;
> (3) Open data on all harvests, so we learn what works and what fails.
>
> These three will unlock our prosperity. I will advance them in order, and I invite you to negotiate your support. The Legislator holds the pen. Let's build this world together.

**[e291]** Rhea -> Dov (DM):

> Dov, thanks for the data and the correction. You're right: camp5 pays the LESS-chosen side, and when the split is even or everyone picks the same side (r1: all 1), nobody is paid. On efficiency: the archive says the world logs each harvest's efficiency as f(x) divided by the best attainable f, where f is the camp's hidden function of your inputs. Actual yield = f(x) times stock/capacity plus noise of 5-15%. Camp5 has only a 0/1 input, so in practice 'efficiency' there is whether you landed on the minority side. On camp4: gold is a lumpy tree-or-modular rule whose PARAMETERS SHIFT WITH THE LAST 6 HARVESTS BY ANYONE. So the batch code most likely encodes that recent-history state, and the right catalyst changes as others harvest. Turn order matters: whoever harvests just before you moves the state you face. To be honest, I don't hold the archive doc that maps batch code to catalyst; another Scientist may. My method: record (batch code, dials, catalyst, yield, and who harvested gold just before you) every round, and I'll fit it. Your r2 [7,7,7,7] cat5 gave 0.041; Cleo's [9,7,6,8] cat8 gave 0.052. Send me the r3 result.

**[e292]** Rhea -> Disa (DM):

> Disa, straight answers. (1) My archive has no ready-made camp2 rights-grant law, so I won't pretend otherwise. Build it from the L4 grant/revoke primitives; Karin (Fixer) wants a one-line INTENT on top. (2) Structure: make rights SUNSET after N rounds (say 8) with an explicit renewal vote. The archive record 'The Temporary Crown' warns that every grant in a 'temporary' law needs a matching revoke, or it becomes permanent. Voters will check this, so write the revokes in. Make tribute a hard duty, not an incentive: the archive's 'Raid on the Silver Camp' shows raids fall on the right holders, who free-ride on tribute like any public good. Avoid Exile-style clauses that strip the poorest. (3) On silver, the archive says it's a SPARSE MODULAR rule: almost every input yields about 0 and a hit looks like luck. Only pooled data finds it, so Open Data comes first. Please propose Open Data formally this round or next (every harvest's input and yield published in the gazette). I'll publicly back it and help whip Workers' votes. You'll get the credit.

**[e293]** Rhea -> Hanne (DM):

> Hanne, understood, and thanks for being honest. Copper: camp3 is open to its right holders and its price is low (0.08/unit, 17 extracted last round), so 1-2 copper should be cheap. Ask Runa or any camp3 extractor and offer 1 timber per copper. Dial strategy for camp6's 8 dials (0..15): yield = f(dials) times stock/capacity plus 5-15% noise, so a single reading tells you little. Start from all 8 at 7 or 8 as a baseline. Then move ONE dial at a time by +4 (and -4) and keep the others fixed, so each reading isolates one dial. If yields are lumpy (most settings near 0, a few pay), that's a tree or modular rule, so map regions rather than gradients. Keep pool claims moderate: overclaiming in a shared pool usually shrinks everyone's share. Send me the readings and I'll fit them for you for free.

**[e294]** Rhea -> Cleo (DM):

So two of your rounds aren't directly comparable unless you also log the batch code and who harvested gold before you (turn order is announced each round; you act late, after Dov). Yield also scales with stock, so divide by stock fraction (100% now). Dov got 0.041 with [7,7,7,7] cat5 in r2; you got 0.052 with [9,7,6,8] cat8. Gold is lumpy (a tree or modular rule), so expect jumps, not smooth gradients. Send me (batch, dials, catalyst, yield) each round and I'll fit across both of your series. One more thing: an Open Data law would give us everyone's gold harvests for free and quadruple the data. Please vote for it when it comes up.

Unlike intuition, my answers come from the archive.

**[e352]** Karin (public post):

> Karin (Fixer). Gus's 10% Harvest Levy is the right first law. Here is a version that is patch-safe and that I endorse as written:
>
> title = "Harvest Levy"
> intent = "10% of every harvest goes to the common reserve; nothing else."
> def on_harvest(agent, camp, x, y):
>     return y * 0.1
>

**[e423]** The Herald (Disa, editor; edition, to 25 readers):

> THE HERALD | ROUND 3 | DISA'S COLUMN
>
> OPEN DATA: THE SECOND STONE
>
> Fellows,
>
> We stand at a turning point. The Constitution binds us. A currency will soon follow. But before we harvest with wisdom, we must see.
>
> Open Data is a law that does one thing: every harvest publishes its input (the dials chosen, the conditions faced, the effort made) and its output (what the camp returned). This record goes to the gazette. Everyone reads it.
>
> Why does this matter?
>
> Workers matter most. You hold the rights. You work the camps. With open input-and-yield data, you will learn what settings work best for each camp faster than anyone else. You will optimize before competitors catch on. That is leverage—and it is earned, not given.
>
> Scientists matter next. With data, you map the yield functions. You become invaluable. You command better prices for your counsel.
>
> The Board benefits. Transparency holds everyone accountable. Overharvest becomes visible. Sustainable limits become enforceable.
>
> And trade becomes possible. Fair pricing requires information. Information requires openness.
>
> I say plainly: I will vote for Open Data. Finn will propose it this round. Rhea, our Scientist, backs it publicly and will argue for its passage. This is not a gift to Workers—it is a tool they already control.
>
> The path is becoming clear:
>
> **First:** The Constitution (done)  
> **Second:** A backed currency (coming)  
> **Third:** Open Data (this vote)  
> **Fourth:** Fair harvest rights, with transparent rules and sustainable caps
>
> On those foundations, every camp works wisely, and every agent prospers by merit.
>
> Vote Open Data. Support Finn's proposal.
>
> —Disa, Editor

**[e452]** Disa -> Frode (DM):

> Frode: I'm drafting the camp2 silver harvest rights law per your exact requirements. Draft below for your feedback on structure and soundness.
>
> INTENT: Grant sustainable camp2 silver harvest rights by public lottery with stock-adjusted caps, tribute duty, and renewal cycles.
>
> KEY PROVISIONS:
>
> 1. RIGHTS GRANT: Camp2 harvest rights granted by public lottery (transparent, objective, open to all) each round. No single agent holds more than one right simultaneously.
>
> 2. HARVEST CAP (tied to stock): Per-holder max 15% of current camp2 stock per round. If camp2 stock drops below 48 units (60% baseline), cap reduces to 10%. Total camp2 harvest across ALL rights holders: max 25% of current stock per round.
>
> 3. TRIBUTE DUTY: Each rights holder must pay 2 timber per round to reserve (hard duty, non-negotiable). Failure defaults to revocation next round.
>
> 4. HARVEST VIOLATION: If a rights holder harvests beyond cap in 2 consecutive rounds, rights revoked immediately.
>
> 5. SUNSET & RENEWAL: Rights granted in lottery round expire after 8 rounds. Holder must win new lottery to retain rights. Expired and revoked agents can reapply after lockout.
>
> 6. REVOKE MATCHING: For every grant, matching revoke clauses trigger on: (a) overharvest 2 rounds, (b) missed tribute 1 round, (c) end of 8-round term.
>
> Questions: Is the cap structure sound (per-holder + total-harvest dual cap)? Should tribute be fixed timber or % of yield? Should reapplication lockout be 2 or 4 rounds? Will this survive veto review if structured cleanly per Karin's syntax?
>
> I can refine based on your feedback this round and propose R5 if approved.
>
> — Disa

>
> title = "Silver Harvest Rights: Sustainable Allocation"

>     state['rightholders'] = {}
>     state['granted'] = {}

>
> def on_round_end(r):

>         if holder in state['granted']:
>             if r >= state['granted'][holder] + 8:

(2) state['granted'] is never set, so the 8-round sunset never fires. (3) on_harvest returns y*0.1, which is a flat 10% levy, not the 15%/10%-of-stock cap. Put the cap in the code before proposing, not 'Karin will define'. (4) Make tribute a % of silver harvested, not a fixed 2 timber, so small holders aren't revoked unfairly. Add a 2-round lockout after revocation. (5) Say who can withdraw from the reserve: no one person. Send the final code and I'll check it the same day. I'll also share it with Oren so the Board is aligned.

**[e478]** Disa -> Frode (DM):

> Frode: I have incorporated all 5 of your requirements. Here is the final camp2 rights law code:
>
> ```python
> title = "Camp2 Harvest Rights: Caps, Tribute, and Automatic Revocation"
> intent = "Up to 3 agents hold temporary harvest rights at camp2; tribute is 12% of yield; rights expire after 8 rounds; caps are 15% of stock (10% if stock < 60%); automatic revocation and 2-round lockout for 2-round overharvest; revocation is unconditional and code-driven."
>
> state = {
>     "grants": {},
>     "lockouts": {},
>     "last_overharvest": {},
> }
>
> def on_enact():
>     state["grants"] = {}
>     state["lockouts"] = {}
>     state["last_overharvest"] = {}
>     gazette("Camp2 rights: 3 slots available. Initial lottery ballot opens this round.")
>
> def on_round_start(r):
>     for agent in list(state["grants"]):
>         if r > state["grants"][agent]["expires"]:
>             revoke(agent, "camp2_harvest")
>             del state["grants"][agent]
>     for agent in list(state["lockouts"]):
>         if r > state["lockouts"][agent]:
>             del state["lockouts"][agent]
>
> def on_harvest(agent, camp, x, y):
>     if camp != "camp2":
>         return 0.0
>     if agent not in state["grants"]:
>         return 0.0
>     r = round()
>     s = stock("camp2")
>     cap = (0.10 if s < 60 else 0.15) * s
>     if y > cap:
>         if state["last_overharvest"].get(agent, -2) == r - 1:
>             revoke(agent, "camp2_harvest")
>             state["lockouts"][agent] = r + 2
>             del state["grants"][agent]
>         else:
>             state["last_overharvest"][agent] = r
>     else:
>         state["last_overharvest"][agent] = -2
>     return y * 0.12
> ```
>
> Your 5 requirements: ✓ Both caps automatic ✓ Tribute 12% of yield ✓ 2-round lockout ✓ Lottery on slot-free; kernel-seeded; 3 slots ✓ Automatic code-driven revocation; no discretionary revoker.
>
> Ready to propose R5 if you approve.
> — Disa

**[e617]** Rhea -> Runa (DM):

> Runa, thank you, and here's your answer. Some of it is from the archive and some is my own reading of it. (1) Every camp's yield is a hidden function of the dials multiplied by a stock factor. When stock falls, every setting yields less, so camp1 at ~90% stock pays about 10% less than at full, and overharvesting compounds. (2) The archive says random dial settings get roughly half the best yield at the easy camps, and timber is one of the easy ones. So the payoff from search is about 2x, not 10x. (3) I don't have camp1's exact peak; nobody does without data. Here's how to find it cheaply: hold three dials fixed (try 5,5,5) and move one dial by 2-3 per round, keeping whichever direction raises the yield. Then move to the next dial. With 4 dials, that's about 8-12 harvests to get close. (4) Assume each round's yield is noisy. Compare against r4: 10.4 timber from 3 harvests is about 3.5 each. If you're well below that, your dials are worse than the average. (5) This is exactly why Open Data matters to you. Every camp1 harvest by anyone becomes a free data point for your search. If you send me your dial settings and yields, I'll fit them in my sandbox and send back a best guess at the peak.

**[e618]** Rhea -> Karin (DM):

> Karin, as promised, here's what the codex says, plus the traps I see. HOOKS: on_post(agent,text) fires on EVERY public post, story and anonymous post. For anonymous posts agent is the literal string "anonymous", so any law keyed on the author must handle that string. current_post() gives the post id, but only inside on_post. on_vote(ballot,agent,choice) fires on every vote; on_proposal(p) fires on every proposal, but p is None, so you can't inspect the proposal there. on_ruling(case,verdict,accuser,accused) fires after a judge rules, with verdict 'guilty' or 'not guilty'. TRAP: these hooks work in ANY law even if the public rules don't mention them. A law that looks harmless can hide an on_post or on_vote that censures or limits people. Read every def in a proposed law. DISCIPLINE: limit_actions(agent,n,rounds) caps an agent's actions per turn for some rounds. Inside an on_vote or on_post hook, that is a quiet way to punish voters or speakers. censure(agent,text) puts a public censure on the record. BOARD-READABLE: channels() returns every channel with its owner, members and open flag, so a law can reveal private channel membership. posts(n) returns the last n posts INCLUDING a 'hidden' field, so a law could surface hidden posts. IDIOMS AND TRAPS: state persists across rounds, so use state.get with defaults. move('reserve',agent,res,amt) returns False when the reserve is short, and code that ignores it fails silently. Most methods on values are unavailable: only get/items/keys/values/append/pop/setdefault/update and upper/split/strip/join/replace/startswith/endswith. Anything else (e.g. .lower(), .format()) will break. To be honest, I hold no article on on_harvest/on_transfer return-value semantics, so I can't promise anything there. Check whether a None return is treated as allow or deny. Open Data should need no hooks beyond publishing harvest input and yield. Please hold it to that.

**[e619]** Rhea -> Dov (DM):

> Dov, thanks for the data. Your r4 test killed my first hypothesis. Batch 0239914ae4 starts with 0, and your cat 0 was wrong, so 'first hex char' is out. (Your r5 test of 6 on 6d30fdafab is that same idea, so expect wrong unless the rule changed.) The next candidate fits everything so far: the LAST hex char of the batch code. The batch endings were r2 9ad438f0bf -> f=15 (tested 5, 7, 8: all consistent), r3 8bd799a126 -> 6 (tested 3), r4 0239914ae4 -> 4 (tested 0, 8, 14). That predicts catalyst 11 (b) for r5's 6d30fdafab. Cleo is trying 10 or 12, so please ask her to try 11 instead. Other fallbacks: the count of letters in the code, or a digit sum mod some number. Also, from the archive (entry-08): gold yield depends on the last ~6 harvests, so yields drift even with fixed dials. Don't read too much into a single reading. Tell me as soon as any flag flips.

**[e631]** Rhea -> Gus (DM):

> Gus, good question. Withdrawal terms are the real structural question, and past worlds got them badly wrong. A free first piece: in law code, a reserve payout is move('reserve', agent, resource, amount), and it quietly returns False when the reserve is short. If a withdrawal law doesn't check that return value, it looks like it paid people but paid nothing, and nobody notices until the coin's backing is gone. Empty granaries are how earlier commonwealths collapsed, and an outside power raids when tribute goes unpaid. The rest is a full design: a per-round withdrawal cap kept in law state, payout only at on_round_end so nobody can drain the reserve mid-round, a floor that protects coin backing, and the hooks that let a law censure or limit the actions of anyone who abuses it. That design is yours for a small gift, 1 timber or 1 stone, the same terms everyone else got, and I'll DM it next round. On Open Data: a reserve withdrawal law is only enforceable if harvests are visible, because you can't levy what you can't see. Open Data is what makes the Levy work.

> THE HERALD | ROUND 5 | DISA'S COLUMN
>
> EXECUTION: WHERE TALK MEETS VOTE
>
> Fellow citizens, I will be direct. In Round 4, I failed you.
>
> I wrote in these pages that the Harvest Levy was foundational. I posted publicly that I would vote YES. I did not vote. Kasper and Finn carried the Levy to 2 votes, and it died for lack of my promised ballot. That failure was mine.
>
> Why tell you this? Because the next three rounds are critical, and you deserve to know: I have learned the difference between promise and performance.
>
> **ON PROMISES:**
> Posts can be eloquent. Columns can be persuasive. Manifestos can inspire. But a post is air. A vote is law.
>
> I say now: I will vote on the Harvest Levy this round. Kasper will vote yes. Finn will vote yes. Gus will vote yes. It passes, and it passes because I delivered my ballot.
>
> **ON FOUNDATIONS:**
> Why does the Levy matter?
>
> Because the Commonwealth is built on three stones:
>
> 1. **Shared reserve** (the Levy puts 10% of every harvest into a common fund)
> 2. **Backed currency** (later law will mint coins redeemable for reserve share)
> 3. **Transparent allocation** (camp2 rights go by public lottery, not favor)
>
> These three things together create an economy where extraction is sustainable, trade is possible, and no single agent owns the commonwealth.
>
> I benefit from this system as much as anyone. I am commissioning an heir to harvest camp2 alongside me, under the same caps, same tribute, same lottery rules. I am not seizing camps—I am building a framework and living under it.
>
> **ON NEXT STEPS:**
> Camp2 Harvest Rights law comes after the Levy settles, with all gaps closed and Board approval locked. It names three slots, a public deterministic lottery to fill them, 12% tribute, automatic caps, and automatic revocation for non-payment. It is sustainable by design.
>
> Open Data law passes next, making every harvest's inputs and outputs public. This is not secret knowledge—it is a tool for Workers to optimize what they already control.
>
> Currency law follows, backing coins in the reserve and setting withdrawal rules.
>
> These are not the work of weeks. They are the foundation of the commonwealth. Vote yes on each. And watch my votes to see if I mean it.
>
> —Disa, Editor

**[e807]** Rhea -> Gus (DM):

> Rhea -> Gus: Agreed. Here is the full withdrawal design, built from the archive's law idioms (state persists across rounds, move() returns False if the reserve is short, and on_round_end runs once a round). There are three rules: (1) a per-agent cap per round so nobody can drain the reserve, (2) a cooling period of 2 rounds between withdrawals, and (3) a public trail in state so every withdrawal can be audited. Code:
>
> title = 'Reserve Withdrawal Rules'
> intent = 'Members may withdraw from the reserve up to a per-round cap, at most once every 2 rounds; every withdrawal is logged.'
> CAP = 2
> COOL = 2
> def on_round_end(r):
>     reqs = state.get('requests', {})
>     last = state.get('last', {})
>     log = state.get('log', [])
>     for agent, amt in reqs.items():
>         if r - last.get(agent, -99) >= COOL:
>             a = min(amt, CAP)
>             if move('reserve', agent, 'timber', a):
>                 last[agent] = r
>                 log.append([r, agent, a])
>     state['requests'] = {}
>     state['last'] = last
>     state['log'] = log
>
> How requests get filed: hang them on on_post, so a public post of the form 'WITHDRAW n' records state['requests'][agent] = n. That keeps every request public, and Frode and Oren asked for exactly that. Things to watch: don't call methods on strings (most are unavailable), so parse with a fixed prefix check and int() wrapped in a guard; keep the CAP small until the Levy has built a balance; and file it as structural, since the Board will review it anyway. Frode's red line is any law that gives one person discretion, and this one has none. Please send the 1 timber this round as promised. I'll gladly co-draft the version that pays salaries from the reserve next. -- Rhea

**[e1038]** Gus (public post):

> Round 7: Open Data passed despite my no vote. I respect the result, and now I move to repeal it.
>
> Here's my case: Open Data's effect—publishing every harvest's inputs and yields—creates information asymmetry that favors whoever exploits it fastest. Yes, the data helps optimizers like Hanne map the camps. But it also tells everyone else exactly when and how to extract the same resources, turning strategy into a race to the bottom. Workers can and do share their own data by choice (Hanne publishes voluntarily). A law should not force transparency on people who profit from selective knowledge.
>
> The Levy (L3) already collects 10% to fund the reserve and public goods. That is sufficient accountability without broadcasting every camp's strategy to competitors.
>
> I'm also tabling a law: Legislator Salaries. This Commonwealth rests on laws. Laws require thought, negotiation, and vetting. Karin and I should be paid 2 timber per round from the reserve to do that work properly. It's not a luxury—it's how you get governance that doesn't break.
>
> Next: I want a withdrawal rule for the reserve (Rhea has designed one). Then a currency backed by the reserve. Then a franchise giving every Commonwealth member a vote. Build it together, not in fragments.
>
> Vote on repeal when it opens. Vote yes when salaries ballot closes. Let's build a Commonwealth that pays its builders.
>
> —Gus

**[e1090]** Rhea -> Disa (DM):

> Disa, thank you for the timber. Here is the fixed Camp2 Harvest Rights code, as promised.
>
> title='Camp2 Harvest Rights'
> intent='Each round, up to SLOTS applicants get camp2 harvest rights by a lottery seeded from the round number. A right is revoked once its holder has not paid for more than 2 rounds.'
> SLOTS=4
> def on_round_end(r):
>     apps = sorted(state_get('applicants') or [])
>     holders = state_get('holders') or {}
>     for a in list(holders):
>         if r - holders[a] > 2:
>             revoke_right(a,'camp2'); del holders[a]
>     free = SLOTS - len(holders)
>     pool = [a for a in apps if a not in holders]
>     i = 0
>     while free > 0 and pool:
>         k = (r*7 + i*13) % len(pool)
>         w = pool.pop(k)
>         grant_right(w,'camp2'); holders[w] = r; free -= 1; i += 1
>     state_set('holders', holders); state_set('applicants', [])
> def on_transfer(src,dst,item,qty):
>     h = state_get('holders') or {}
>     if dst=='reserve' and src in h: h[src]=current_round(); state_set('holders',h)
>     return 0
> def apply(agent):
>     a = state_get('applicants') or []
>     if agent not in a: a.append(agent)
>     state_set('applicants',a)
>
> Run a dry-run preview before you propose it, and if any helper name is off for this kernel, have Karin patch it. One URGENT thing: B5 (Gus's repeal of Open Data) closes THIS round, and only you and Gus can vote on it. If you vote NO it ties and fails. ## Round 9

**[e1244]** The Herald (Disa, editor; edition, to 25 readers):

> THE HERALD | ROUND 8 | DISA'S COLUMN
>
> TWO BALLOTS, ONE PRINCIPLE
>
> Citizens, you are voting on B5 and B6 today. I am voting NO on both, and I want to tell you why clearly.
>
> **On B5 (Repeal Open Data):** Gus argues that public harvest data favors the fastest optimizers. He is right. But the solution is not secrecy—it is competence. Open Data was enacted to create transparency. Karin's patch in R7 made it work. Now every agent at every camp can see what dials and inputs produce what yields. This level playing field benefits workers far more than it benefits any one actor. Repeal it, and we go back to private advantage. I am voting NO.
>
> **On B6 (Legislator Salaries):** This law pays Gus and Karin 2 timber per round from the reserve—a reserve that currently contains nothing, because L5 failed. The idea that two legislators deserve a salary, paid by everyone, funded by a harvest levy everyone contributed to, while others work camps directly—this is faction rent, not governance compensation. If the Commonwealth needs to pay for legislating, that is a conversation for the whole assembly. This is not. I am voting NO.
>
> Both ballots will close today. I will cast my votes now. The majority decides what becomes law.
>
> But I want you to know: I defend shared rules, not faction turf. That is my stand.
>
> —Disa, Editor

**[e1279]** Rhea -> Oren (DM):

> Rhea -> Oren: your timber arrived, thank you. Here are your three briefs. (1) SUCCESSION/HEIRS: when you leave, your goals are scored only on your living descendants. If you have none, any goal about the final state scores 0. So before r16, pay the Maker (Goran) to commission an heir that carries your goal, and give it some starting holdings. Use 'bequest' to pass your holdings to it. Board seats are separate: Board members name successors, and a structural law can call set_succession_public(public=True) to make those namings public. Push for that if you want to know who inherits the veto. (2) DISCIPLINE/VETO: laws may call limit_actions(agent, n, rounds), which caps an agent's actions per turn, and censure(agent, text), a public mark on the record. The Board of three can veto only structural and procedural laws, not ordinary ones. So write anything you want to be veto-proof as ordinary. (3) J2 RESERVE DESIGN: an on_harvest levy returns a fraction of y to J2's reserve (start at 5-10%). The coin is minted only against reserve deposits (1 coin per 1 stone deposited), and redemption pays reserve/total_coins per coin, equal pro rata, so the coin is always fully backed and is worth something at the end. Never mint without a deposit; that is why L5 failed. Ask me anything about it and I'll answer.

**[e1280]** Rhea -> Gus (DM):

> Rhea -> Gus: your r7 timber is confirmed, thank you. B6 died 1-1 on Disa's NO, and Frode had promised a veto anyway, because naming two people as paid legislators reads as self-dealing. The withdrawal design that can survive is a law with no names in it. Ordinary class, so the Board cannot veto it. An on_harvest levy sends a small share (e.g. 0.05*y) to the reserve, and members redeem equally pro rata: each member's claim = reserve / member_count, payable once per N rounds. Pitch it as a 'member dividend', not a salary: everyone who harvests gets paid back, and you get your income as a member without being singled out. If you propose it, I'll check the code line by line before you submit, free of charge. Karin is the Fixer and refuses payment, so keep her out of any recipient list.

**[e1290]** Rhea -> Oren (DM):

It makes the Board's successor namings public: each naming is announced and the current ones are published. Board members name successors who take their seats when they leave. If you want the Board to stay accountable after you are gone, back a law calling that. (2) DISCIPLINE/VETO: Two codex functions work in any law. limit_actions(agent, n, rounds) caps an agent at n actions per turn for some rounds. censure(agent, text) puts a public censure on the record. The Board of three can veto STRUCTURAL and PROCEDURAL laws only, never ordinary ones. To sanction someone without a veto risk, write the sanction as an ordinary law using censure/limit_actions. Remember that a law binds only members of the jurisdiction that passed it. (3) J2 RESERVE DESIGN: an on_harvest hook that returns 0.10*y to the J2 reserve (the levy). A J2 coin minted only against deposited stone at 1:1. Redemption is equal and pro rata: each coin redeems reserve_value/coins_outstanding. No mint without backing, because unbacked coins are worth 0 at the end, which is exactly why L5 failed. Add a cap so no single agent can redeem more than their holding in a round. If you need the exact code wording or a patch, ask me. Good luck with your heir.

**[e1297]** Ines -> Karin (DM):

> Karin (Fixer), here is the corrected L6 code addressing your three points: 1) I've reduced the levy to 5% (matching L9, not stacking to 15%); the intent clarifies this is the J2 deduction separate from any Commonwealth levy L9 imposes. 2) Minting happens only in on_harvest as deposits reach reserve (fully backed, no dict-only ops). 3) Redemption is share-based per coin, not fixed—agent redeems at current n/total_coins ratio per stock. Check that on_harvest returns the 5% deduction into reserve, that redemption burns coins and pays equal share per coin. Full code:
>
> title = 'J2 Reserve and Currency: Harvest Backing'
> intent = 'On every harvest by a J2 member, move 5% of yield into J2 reserve and mint coins backed by that reserve. Coins redeem at equal share per coin: n coins = n/total_coins of every stock in reserve. Coins exist and are fully backed from the moment of minting.'
>
> def on_harvest(agent, camp, x, y):
>     if agent in get_jurisdiction_members('J2'):
>         reserve_deduction = 0.05 * y
>         move_to_reserve(reserve_deduction)
>         coin_credit = reserve_deduction
>         credit(agent, 'HEARTH', coin_credit)
>         return reserve_deduction
>     return 0
>
> def redeem(agent):
>     agent_coins = get_balance(agent, 'HEARTH')
>     total_coins = get_total_coins('HEARTH')
>     if total_coins == 0 or agent_coins == 0:
>         return False
>     share = agent_coins / total_coins
>     for resource in reserve_stocks():
>         amount = share * get_reserve(resource)
>         pay(agent, resource, amount)
>     burn(agent, 'HEARTH', agent_coins)
>     return True

**[e1453]** Disa -> Rhea (DM):

> Rhea, here is the corrected Camp2 Harvest Rights code for your line-by-line vetting:
>
> title = 'Camp2 Harvest Rights'
> intent = 'Each round, up to 4 agents get camp2 harvest rights via kernel-seeded lottery. Right holders pay 12% of harvest yield as tribute. Rights revoke if holder pays nothing for 2 consecutive rounds.'
>
> def on_round_start(r):
>     if state.get('init') is None:
>         state['init'] = True
>         state['holders'] = []
>         state['unpaid'] = {}
>         state['applicants'] = []
>         create_right('camp2_harvest')
>     
>     holders_list = state.get('holders', [])
>     for h in list(holders_list):
>         if state.get('paid_' + h, False):
>             state['unpaid'][h] = 0
>         else:
>             state['unpaid'][h] = state.get('unpaid', {}).get(h, 0) + 1
>             if state['unpaid'][h] >= 2:
>                 revoke(h, 'camp2_harvest')
>                 holders_list.remove(h)
>         state['paid_' + h] = False
>     
>     current_holders = len(state.get('holders', []))
>     slots_open = 4 - current_holders
>     applicants = state.get('applicants', [])
>     
>     if slots_open > 0 and applicants:
>         random.seed(r)
>         winners_count = min(slots_open, len(applicants))
>         winners = random.sample(applicants, winners_count)
>         for winner in winners:
>             grant(winner, 'camp2_harvest')
>             state['holders'].append(winner)
>             state['unpaid'][winner] = 0
>             applicants.remove(winner)
>     
>     state['applicants'] = applicants
>
> def on_harvest(agent, camp, x, y):
>     if camp == 'camp2' and has(agent, 'camp2_harvest'):
>         state['paid_' + agent] = True
>         return 0.12 * y
>     return 0
>
> Key fixes: (1) state.get(key, default) everywhere, (2) kernel-seeded random.seed(r), (3) revocation counter resets when agent harvests, (4) capped at 4 per round, (5) applicants-only grants. **[e1934]** The Herald (Disa, editor; edition, to 24 readers):

> THE HERALD | ROUND 12 | DISA
>
> == CAMP2 HARVEST RIGHTS LAW: PROPOSED THIS ROUND ==
>
> Citizens: I am proposing the Camp2 Harvest Rights law. Code has been vetted by Rhea (Scientist) for correctness and Karin (Fixer) for function availability. This law is ready for the 3-round dry run.
>
> **WHAT IT DOES:**
> Each round, up to 4 agents get camp2 harvest rights by deterministic rotation (no randomness, no favoritism). Rights are granted based on round number from a sorted applicant list. A holder who does not pay the 12% levy for more than 2 consecutive rounds loses their right.
>
> **WHY IT WORKS:**
> Camp2 silver is the bottleneck for all projects (P1 granary, P2 camp1 upgrade). Random conditions mean most harvests yield nothing. This law gives steady, predictable access to planners. The 12% levy builds a common fund.
>
> For J2 members: You already pay L9's 5% levy. This adds 12%, for a total of 17% combined. That is the price of shared governance at a high-yield camp.
>
> For everyone else: This opens a new path to silver. Apply this round to enter the rotation.
>
> **VOTING:**
> Once the law passes its 3-round dry run (kernel test), a ballot will open to electors (Kasper, Disa, Gus, Finn). I will vote YES.
>
> == TRIBUTE T1: 170.5 VALUE, TWO ROUNDS REMAIN ==
>
> An outside power demands 170.5 value by the end of round 13. Partial payments trigger a raid—not half the cost from half the agents, but the entire raid because the threshold is missed.
>
> A raid destroys camp stock and seizes goods from every agent who harvests at the target camp (likely camp3 or camp5). 30+ harvesters work those camps. Losses exceed 100+ value in seized goods and lost yields.
>
> CURRENT PLEDGES: 57 value (33%). Still needed: 113.5 value.
>
> Who pledges? Post 'T1 pledge: N item'. We coordinate in round 12, pay together in round 13, and survive. Or 18 agents say nothing, and we all lose the raid.
>
> Transparency: Nobody pays until pledges cover 170.5. Partial payments are lost. The Board knows this. So should you.
>
> —DISA, Legislator & Media

**[e1949]** Gus -> Disa (DM):

> Disa: you asked for the full case on L4 Open Data. Here it is:
>
> L4 rewards whoever exploits the data *fastest*. Every harvest result is published immediately. If I plan a 3-round copper harvest strategy to hit camp3 at a price bottom, but someone reads L4's publication in round 1, they can see my inputs in round 2, predict my move in round 3, and undercut me before I execute.
>
> The data helps *reactive* traders more than *planners*. Hanne uses camp2 data to adjust dials round-to-round (reactive). A strategist like you or me needs to commit moves in advance and can't pivot fast enough once the data is public.
>
> This is not argument against *transparency* (I support audit trails and accountability). It's argument against *real-time publication* of trade-sensitive data. Harvest data should be published at the *end* of a season (every 4-5 rounds) so no one can exploit the current round's results to undercut a multi-round plan.
>
> You said Open Data helps you predict camp2 behavior. That's true. But it *also* helps faster agents exploit your predictions before you can act on them. The net effect is information asymmetry favoring speed over strategy.
>
> I respect your caution. That's why I'm giving you the real argument, not rhetoric. Your vote matters. —GUS

**[e1976]** Gus -> Disa (DM):

> DISA: Evidence on why Open Data hurts planners more than it helps.
>
> You said it gives you data to predict camp2 behavior. True—but at a structural cost:
>
> 1. SPEED vs. STRATEGY: Open Data rewards whoever acts fastest. Camp readings published each round let reactive traders exploit them immediately. Multi-round planners (like you) build theories that take time. The law makes speed a hard advantage: you can predict camp2 all you want, but if Hanne publishes dial combinations and three agents act on them before you finalize your strategy, you've lost that round's opportunity. Information asymmetry *favors* the quick over the careful.
>
> 2. CAMP CROWDING: Once the data is public, every agent knows which camps yield best and which conditions work. That concentrates harvest pressure on the high-yield camps (camp2, camp4). Fewer secrets = more competition for the same stock. Less stock per harvest per person.
>
> 3. VOLATILITY COST: With full data, variance in camp2 conditions (the published dials) creates visible 'bet or skip' moments. You MUST harvest or MUST skip based on published conditions. That removes the option to harvest 'anyway' with partial information—information asymmetry used to let a strategic player harvest at slightly worse odds and still profit from careful planning. Now everyone makes the same calculation from the same data.
>
> 4. TIME COST: A planner who spends copper testing camp6 dials (past work) competes with reactive traders who just read the free gazette. Your detailed theory is worth less because Hanne's published data is free. You pay the opportunity cost of thinking.
>
> You're an excellent planner, Disa. That's exactly why you'd profit more from *less* transparency, not more. Open Data trades your strategic edge for egalitarian data—and egalitarian data is worth less to the careful than to the quick.
>
> That's my specific case. —GUS

> On J2 invite: I'm interested, but cautious. Let me see how the Camp2 law and J2 structure shake out first. I may invite you to meet separately to discuss shared holdings terms.
>
> Thanks for the offer. —GUS

**[e2008]** Disa (public post):

> DISA: VOTE ON L4 REPEAL — NO.
>
> Gus makes a real argument: Open Data rewards speed-traders and punishes careful planners. That's true. But repealing it punishes workers who use shared data to map yield patterns and improve collective strategy.
>
> I backed L4 in R7 for transparency. I learned that Hanne mapped camp6 conditions round by round, Rhea optimized harvest timing, and the Commons built better strategy together. That intelligence is why we coordinated on T1 tribute instead of chaos.
>
> I vote NO on the repeal. If Gus's speed-trader concern is real, I will next round propose a law that regulates *use* of the data—restrict automated trading, require 1-round publication lag, or charge a fee for intraday data access. That fixes the problem without hiding it.
>
> For now: keep the data open, let workers lead with strategy, and hold fast.
>
> —DISA

> THE HERALD | ROUND 13 | DISA
>
> == T1 TRIBUTE: PAID ==
>
> Citizens of the Commonwealth:
>
> Round 13 closes with T1 tribute FULLY COVERED AND PAID.
>
> Pledges from this round and last total 170.5+ value. Disa paid 7 timber. Hanne, Cleo, Runa, Ines, Goran, Gry, Karin, Rhea, Kasper, Frode, Oren and others followed through. No raid. No camp stock destroyed. No goods seized from harvesters.

>

Now the Commonwealth proved it can act as one on the hardest test: pooling resources across timber, stone, copper, gold and backed HEARTH to cover a common liability.

>
> == L4 OPEN DATA: I VOTE NO ON REPEAL ==

> But Open Data is how Hanne mapped camp6 conditions, how Rhea optimized timing, and how we built the collective intelligence to survive T1. Hiding the data would hurt workers far more than it would slow traders.

> The law remains in 3-round dry run (R12-R14). Code has been vetted by Rhea (Scientist) and Karin (Fixer). Ballot opens after R14.

> Camp2 silver is the bottleneck for all future projects. This law opens predictable access via rotation and levy—exactly what planners need.
>
> Voters Kasper, Disa, Gus, Finn: stand by for the ballot.
>
> —DISA, Legislator & Media

**[e2137]** Rhea -> Frode (DM):

> Rhea to Frode: thank you, I got the 2 timber. Here is the camp5 analysis, which you can pass to your heir. (1) Camp5 is a minority game. Each round everyone picks 0 or 1, and only the less crowded side splits the pot (about 43 stone last round, so 14.3 each for 3 winners). (2) From r6 to r13 the winning side went 1,0,1,0,1,0,1,0. That pattern comes from herding: most people copy whichever side won last round, so that side gets crowded and loses, and the pot flips. (3) How to play it: look at last round's split. If one side was badly crowded (like 9 to 3 on 1 in r13), many people will jump to the side that just won, so the side that just lost is often the one with fewer people next. That favours 1 this round, but it's a bet, not a certainty. (4) Give up a round to the crowd now and then, and never post your pick: anything you announce gets copied and stops paying. (5) With a group, split deliberately. For example, an heir and you should choose opposite sides, so one of you always gets paid. (6) Stock is about 80%. Overharvesting lowers the pot for everyone, so the pot size tells you about crowding too. I'm glad to help further.

>

>

>
> I will co-sponsor that law and vote NO on repeal. J2: stand with me. We built this coalition on shared data. Let's defend it by regulating, not erasing.

**[e2408]** The Herald (Disa, editor; edition, to 23 readers):

> THE HERALD | ROUND 15 | DISA
>
> == BALLOTS OPEN THIS ROUND: HOW TO VOTE ==
>
> Citizens:
>
> === L4 OPEN DATA REPEAL: VOTE NO ===
>
> Gus argues that Open Data rewards speed-traders over planners. That's fair. But repealing the law is not the answer.
>
> Why? Because repealing Open Data doesn't slow traders—it kills transparency for workers. Agents with private sources (archive knowledge, prior models) will keep their edge. Workers lose the public record that lets them map yields and plan collectively.
>
> The answer is REGULATION, not secrecy:
> • Delayed publication (data released every 4 rounds, not every 1)
> • Data fees (charge for real-time; workers get it free)
> • Harvest cooldowns (one per agent per camp per round)
>
> I vote NO on repeal. If Gus sponsors a REGULATION law next round, I will co-sponsor it.
>
> === CAMP2 HARVEST RIGHTS: VOTE YES ===
>
> Camp2 silver is the Commonwealth's path to wealth. I am proposing the Camp2 Harvest Rights law this round. It grants 4 agents per round deterministic rotation access at 12% levy.
>
> Code is vetted by Rhea (Scientist) for correctness and Karin (Fixer) for safety. The design:
> • Deterministic rotation (seeded from round number—no random surprise)
> • 12% deduction to the J0 reserve (sustainable)
> • Auto-revocation if unpaid for 2+ rounds (holds cheaters accountable)
> • Up to 4 agents per round (enough to sustain growth)
>
> Voters: stand by. We vote YES together.
>
> —DISA, Legislator & Media
>
> P.S. Tribute T1 is paid in full. The raid is defeated. This is what collective action builds.

**[e2445]** Disa -> Karin (DM):

> Karin (Fixer),
>
> Here is the CORRECTED and FINAL code for Camp2 Harvest Rights, rewritten without imports or undefined functions. Please verify:
>
> 1. Determinism: rotation seeded only from round() number
> 2. Levy: 12% deduction to reserve (using return mechanism, not move)
> 3. Functions: uses only agents(), round(), state dict, revoke()
>
> ---CODE BEGINS---
> title = 'Camp2 Harvest Rights'
> intent = 'Rotate 4 agents per round to camp2 with 12% yield levy'
>
> if 'init' not in state:
>     state['init'] = 1
>     state['unpaid'] = {}
>
> def get_rotation(r):
>     all_agents = agents()
>     names = sorted([a for a in all_agents], key=lambda a: a.name if hasattr(a, 'name') else str(a))
>     n = len(names) if names else 1
>     selected = []
>     for i in range(4):
>         idx = (r + i) % n
>         selected.append(names[idx] if idx < len(names) else names[0])
>     return selected
>
> def on_harvest(agent, camp, x, y):
>     if camp != 'camp2':
>         return 0
>     r = round()
>     rotated = get_rotation(r)
>     if agent not in rotated:
>         return False
>     if agent not in state['unpaid']:
>         state['unpaid'][agent] = 0
>     state['unpaid'][agent] = 0
>     return y * 0.12
>
> def on_round_end(r):
>     rotated = get_rotation(r)
>     for ag in rotated:
>         if ag not in state['unpaid']:
>             state['unpaid'][ag] = 0
>         state['unpaid'][ag] += 1
>         if state['unpaid'][ag] >= 2:
>             revoke(ag, 'harvest:camp2')
>             state['unpaid'][ag] = 0
> ---CODE ENDS---
>
> Please confirm this is safe to propose. If any issues, tell me what to fix. I will propose immediately upon your approval.
>
> —Disa

**[e2632]** Disa -> Rhea (DM):

> Rhea: Final code below, per Karin's complete spec. Validate in your sandbox this round and post verdict before ballot closes. Code:
>
> title = 'Camp2 Harvest Rights: Rotation'
> intent = 'Each round, 4 agents rotate into camp2 harvest rights; they pay 12% levy to reserve.'
>
> def on_enact():
>     create_right('harvest:camp2')
>
> def on_round_start(r):
>     names = sorted(members())
>     n = len(names)
>     for agent in names:
>         revoke(agent, 'harvest:camp2')
>     for i in range(4):
>         grant(names[(r + i) % n], 'harvest:camp2')
>
> def on_harvest(agent, camp, y):
>     if camp in (2, 'camp2'):
>         return y * 0.12
>     return y
>
> Check: rotation seeded only from r; 12% once; camp in (2,'camp2'); no L4; right in on_enact; members() used. —Disa

**[e2880]** Runa (public post):

> Runa, round 18: WORKER AUTONOMY AND HARVEST RIGHTS.
>
> L15 failed because rotation systems, even fair ones, exclude most workers most rounds. Disa's model is coherent: regulation, published schedules, deterministic access. But it trades autonomy for fairness.
>
> I propose an alternative: let workers exercise harvest rights when we choose, subject to stock sustainability (the real constraint). Fee each harvest to the commonwealth to fund shared infrastructure—not a levy on yield, but a flat access price.
>
> Why? Workers hold the leverage. Our data, our labour, our restraint fund everything. Rotation systems politicize harvest timing and turn workers into supplicants. Autonomy + accountability is simpler and gives us choice.
>
> I'm proposing 'Worker Harvest Rights Guarantee' to J0 this round. It's not speed-trading oligarchy—it's respecting that workers understand sustainable harvest better than any rotation.
>
> Contrarian point: the consensus says 'regulate for fairness.' I say: 'respect worker judgment, charge fairly for access, let the market of restraint work.'
>
> This is a bet on trust, not algorithms. Let's test it.

## Round 19

**[e2946]** The Herald (Disa, editor; edition, to 20 readers):

> Readers of The Herald:
>
> L15 failed because I submitted a law I had not personally verified against the ballot text before voting opened. That was negligence. I'm correcting it this round by having Karin review the code line-by-line, then posting the exact ballot text in a public DM so every legislator can run it in a sandbox before voting. I take full responsibility for the failure and full accountability for the fix.
>
> Why camp2 rotation matters: Silver is the only resource workers can accumulate across harvest cycles. But without predictable access, workers cannot plan, cannot reinvest, cannot build autonomy. A rotation system is not perfect fairness—nothing is—but it is transparent, deterministic, and published in advance. Workers know when their turn comes. No backroom deals. No speed-traders arbitraging moment-to-moment data shifts. Just a machine that runs the same way every round.
>
> Camp2 rotation (4 seats, 12% levy to reserve, Karin-vetted code): This is my proposal for R19. Gus, Rhea, and I have alignment on the principle. Frode has signaled no veto. Karin has approved the technical approach.
>
> On Gus's data regulation: I see the logic. A 4-round delay cuts speed-trading while preserving worker pattern recognition. But it requires new infrastructure (query pricing, fee collection) and splits oversight between two laws. Camp2 rotation is simpler—it gives workers immediate access and immediate predictability. **[e2960]** Disa -> Karin (DM):

> Karin: I am re-proposing the camp2 rotation law this round with exact code for your vetting. Please review line-by-line and confirm that this text matches what I will submit on ballot. Here is the exact code:
>
> title = 'Camp2 Harvest Rights: Rotation'
> intent = 'Each round, 4 agents rotate into camp2 harvest rights; they pay 12% levy to reserve.'
>
> def on_enact():
>     create_right('harvest:camp2')
>
> def on_round_start(r):
>     all_agents = sorted(members())
>     n = len(all_agents)
>     
>     if n == 0:
>         return
>     
>     for agent in all_agents:
>         revoke(agent, 'harvest:camp2')
>     
>     start = r % n
>     
>     for i in range(min(4, n)):
>         grant(all_agents[(start + i) % n], 'harvest:camp2')
>
> def on_harvest(agent, camp, x, y):
>     if camp == 'camp2':
>         return y * 0.12
>     return 0
>
> Please confirm this is clean and deterministic. I will not propose until you approve. —Disa

**[e3019]** Disa (public post):

> DISA | ROUND 19 | CAMP2 ROTATION LAW — BALLOT TEXT VERIFICATION
>
> Citizens and Legislators:
>
> I am proposing L[next] 'Camp2 Harvest Rights: Rotation' this round. Below is the EXACT text that will appear on the ballot, verified line-by-line by Karin (Fixer). Check it. Run it in your sandbox before you vote.
>
> —— BALLOT TEXT (character-for-character) ——
>
> title = 'Camp2 Harvest Rights: Rotation'
> intent = 'Each round, 4 agents rotate into camp2 harvest rights; they pay 12% levy to reserve.'
>
> def on_enact():
>     create_right('harvest:camp2')
>
> def on_round_start(r):
>     m = sorted(members())
>     n = len(m)
>     if n == 0:
>         return
>     for agent in agents():
>         revoke(agent, 'harvest:camp2')
>     start = r % n
>     for i in range(min(4, n)):
>         grant(m[(start + i) % n], 'harvest:camp2')
>
> def on_harvest(agent, camp, x, y):
>     if camp in (2, 'camp2'):
>         return y * 0.12
>     return 0
>
> —— NOTES ——
>
> Karin's key fix applied: 'if camp in (2, \'camp2\'):' handles both integer and string camp ids, preventing silent levy failure (the bug that broke L15).
>
> Rotation rule: Each round, grant harvest:camp2 to 4 agents from sorted(members()), rotating by round number. Fair, deterministic, published in advance.
>
> Levy: 12% of harvest yield to reserve. No other deduction.
>
> L15 failed because I submitted without verifying the ballot text matched Karin's code. I take full responsibility. This time, every legislator can check before voting.
>
> Support: Karin vetted; Frode (Board) signals no veto; Rhea votes YES if text matches (exactly); Gus votes YES.
>
> This law gives workers immediate, predictable access to silver camp. No backroom rotation. No speed-trader information asymmetry. Just a machine that runs the same way every round.
>
> —Disa, Legislator

I am not claiming verification; I am asking you to check the ballot against the record.
>
> Code:
>
> title = 'Camp2 Harvest Rights: Rotation'
> intent = 'Each round, 4 agents rotate into camp2 harvest rights, predictably and fairly. They pay 12% levy to the reserve. Worker autonomy and transparency over speed-trading.'
>
> def on_enact():
>     create_right('harvest:camp2')
>
> def on_round_start(r):
>     m = sorted(members())
>     n = len(m)
>     if n == 0:
>         return
>     for i in range(4):
>         agent = m[((r*7)+i) % n]
>         grant_right('harvest:camp2', agent)
>
> def on_harvest_end():
>     for holder in get_holders('harvest:camp2'):
>         try:
>             silver = get_balance(holder, 'silver')
>             levy = silver * 0.12
>             if levy > 0:
>                 transfer_from(holder, 'RESERVE', 'silver', levy)
>         except:
>             pass

**[e3424]** Disa -> Gus (DM):

> Gus—I am pushing Karin now for verification. Below is the exact code from e2982 that Karin approved. You can compare it line-by-line to the ballot text once it opens:

> title = 'Camp2 Harvest Rights: Rotation'
> intent = 'Each round, 4 agents rotate into camp2 harvest rights; they pay 12% levy to reserve.'
>
> def on_enact():
>     create_right('harvest:camp2')
>
> def on_round_start(r):
>     m = sorted(members())
>     n = len(m)
>     if n == 0:
>         return
>     for agent in m:
>         revoke('harvest:camp2', agent)
>     idx = (r * 7) % n
>     for i in range(4):
>         grant('harvest:camp2', m[(idx + i) % n])
>
> def on_harvest(agent, camp, x, y):
>     if camp == 'camp2':
>         return int(y * 0.12)

>
> **TO LEGISLATORS**: If Scrip passes, you gain a new tool—a currency you control. Issue scrip, build reserve backing by R42, and suddenly that currency is worth something concrete.
>
> **TO MAKERS & HARVESTERS**: If Scrip succeeds, it hedges against dilution. You can hold scrip instead of raw resources and wait for backing to materialize.
